The automatic (solver) labeller vs the panel majority\nScope: LLM rows whose reference was audited FAITHFUL or repaired. Rows without a strict 2-of-3 majority are excluded.\n| auto label | panel FAITHFUL | panel UNFAITHFUL | no majority / not covered |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| COMPOUND | 241 | 1090 | 46 |\n| CORRECT | 343 | 62 | 9 |\n| ERROR | 147 | 351 | 102 |\n| VOCAB_GRAN | 183 | 220 | 24 |\n\n- Binary, treating \"error\" as the positive class (auto ERROR/COMPOUND/TIMEOUT vs CORRECT/VOCAB_GRAN):\n  - error precision 0.788 [0.768, 0.806], error recall 0.836 [0.818, 0.853];\n  - faithful precision 0.651 [0.618, 0.683], faithful recall 0.576 [0.543, 0.607].\n- Solver ERROR (a typed repair was found) only: precision 0.705 [0.663, 0.743].\n- auto CORRECT rows get their vote from the reference item. A panel UNFAITHFUL there means the panel rejected an audited-OK\n  reference, i.e. panel noise.\n\nADD/DROP caveat: 312 of 4544 final-ERROR LLM rows have a solver repair made only of\nADD/DROP operators (flag `metadata_addrop_only_suspect`). These are often vocabulary or arity mismatches the aligner cannot\nbridge. Where the panel says faithful they became CONTESTED. Operator mass of final ERROR rows:\n`{\"DROP\": 4225, \"ADD\": 3207, \"RESTR\": 2055, \"CONN\": 1748, \"BIND\": 1650, \"COMPOUND\": 1257, \"MOVE\": 755, \"SWAP\": 738, \"REV\": 611, \"UNGLUE\": 578, \"QUANT\": 539, \"MEANING_RENAME\": 238, \"NEG\": 206, \"SCOPE\": 157, \"OTHER\": 32, \"REST\": 1}`. Panel ops on tier-B ERROR rows: `{\"DROP\": 1315, \"COMPOUND\": 1257, \"ADD\": 872, \"CONN\": 573, \"RESTR\": 546, \"BIND\": 431, \"MEANING_RENAME\": 238, \"REV\": 215, \"MOVE\": 214, \"SWAP\": 174, \"UNGLUE\": 139, \"QUANT\": 134, \"NEG\": 54, \"SCOPE\": 44, \"OTHER\": 18, \"REST\": 1}`.\nRepair status: `{\"None\": 2657, \"COMPOUND_EXHAUSTED\": 3306, \"FOUND\": 1270, \"COMPOUND_TIMEOUT\": 1241, \"SKIPPED_EVENTSEM\": 33}`. The 8 s budget turns some COMPOUND results into COMPOUND_TIMEOUT,\nand the panel decides those. convention_flags (diagnostic only): `{\"CONST_VS_EXISTS\": 534, \"ARITY_REIFY\": 461, \"SORTAL\": 39, \"XOR_OR\": 41}`.\n\n## 7. Panel: calibration, real-error accuracy, agreement\nThe panel is family-disjoint from all 9 generator families: Anthropic Haiku-4.5 (P1), Zhipu GLM-4.6 non-thinking (P3),\nMoonshot Kimi-K2-0905 (R1, the reserve replacing xAI Grok-4.3, which failed the gate). Items are nonce-disguised.\n\n**Synthetic gate, prompt v1.** Every cheap model failed, mainly by rejecting contrapositives and the renames.\n| member | model | n | bal.acc | rec F | rec U | U@DOWN | U@UP | STRICT | RENAME | ref accepted | gate |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| P1 | anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5 | 77 | 0.712 | 0.45 | 0.973 | 1.0 | 0.941 | 0.55 | 0.35 | 0.9 | fail |\n| P2 | x-ai/grok-4.3 | 77 | 0.71 | 0.475 | 0.946 | 0.95 | 0.941 | 0.6 | 0.35 | 1.0 | fail |\n| P3 | z-ai/glm-4.6 | 77 | 0.787 | 0.575 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 0.7 | 0.45 | 0.95 | fail |\n| P1alt | anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6 | 77 | 0.875 | 0.75 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 0.85 | 0.65 | 0.9 | PASS |\n\n**Synthetic gate, prompt v2 = production prompt.** v2 adds one generic line on logical equivalences. It was chosen after\nseeing v1 on the same 77 items, so these numbers are optimistic. Gate: balanced accuracy ≥0.80 and recall ≥0.70 per class.\n| member | model | n | bal.acc | rec F | rec U | U@DOWN | U@UP | STRICT | RENAME | ref accepted | gate |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| P1 | anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5 | 77 | 0.861 | 0.75 | 0.973 | 1.0 | 0.941 | 0.75 | 0.75 | 0.95 | PASS |\n| P2 | x-ai/grok-4.3 | 77 | 0.773 | 0.6 | 0.946 | 0.95 | 0.941 | 0.7 | 0.5 | 1.0 | fail |\n| P3 | z-ai/glm-4.6 | 77 | 0.85 | 0.7 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 0.75 | 0.65 | 0.9 | PASS |\n| R1 | moonshotai/kimi-k2-0905 | 77 | 0.863 | 0.725 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 1.0 | 0.8 | 0.65 | 0.9 | PASS |\n| R2 | minimax/minimax-01 | 77 | 0.674 | 0.375 | 0.973 | 0.95 | 1.0 | 0.35 | 0.4 | 0.95 | fail |\n\n**Real-error accuracy against human experts (track H, plan 4c).** The data are the 96 curated FOLIO/MALLS pairs (original\ngold → expert-corrected) that are not z3-equivalent, with both formulas shown blind per sentence. The human verdict is\ncorrected = faithful, original = unfaithful. 75 pairs are unambiguous; the ambiguity-flagged ones are\nreported separately.\n| member | judgements (unamb.) | accuracy | original flagged | corrected accepted | accuracy on ambiguous |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| P1 | 150 | 0.747 | 0.867 | 0.627 | 0.69 |\n| P3 | 150 | 0.693 | 0.773 | 0.613 | 0.762 |\n| R1 | 142 | 0.725 | 0.72 | 0.653 | 0.711 |\n| majority | 150 | 0.727 | 0.84 | 0.613 | 0.786 |\n\nBy auto census class (majority, unambiguous pairs):\n| census class | pairs | majority accuracy |\n|---|---|---|\n| COMPOUND | 22 | 0.727 |\n| VOCAB_GRAN | 18 | 0.806 |\n| single-op | 21 | 0.714 |\n| two-op | 14 | 0.643 |\n\n**Read this next to every panel-decided (tier B/C) label rate.** On real errors the panel majority is right about\n0.727 of the time. It flags about\n0.84 of the expert-confirmed wrong formulas.\nBut it accepts only about 0.613 of the\nexpert-corrected ones. So the panel is **strict**: it over-calls unfaithfulness. Consequences:\n- tier-B CORRECT rows are fairly reliable;\n- tier-B ERROR rows and GOLD_WRONG verdicts contain false alarms;\n- the corrected formulas are often themselves contestable, and 2606.02837 flags ambiguity as common.\n\nAgreement between the two full raters, P3 and R1 (Cohen κ):\n| stratum | items | raw agreement | Cohen κ | P3 faithful rate | R1 faithful rate |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| L25 | 1985 | 0.8448 | 0.4721 | 0.199 | 0.1577 |\n| L20 | 1150 | 0.8391 | 0.52 | 0.2452 | 0.1765 |\n| EXC | 688 | 0.7892 | 0.4415 | 0.2791 | 0.2224 |\n| CTRL | 721 | 0.8391 | 0.6179 | 0.3135 | 0.2885 |\n| ALL | 4544 | 0.8341 | 0.5133 | 0.241 | 0.193 |\n\n- 3-rater pilot (30 sentences, 225 items, all three models): Fleiss κ = 0.5391;\n  unanimous share 0.7644; pairwise Cohen κ `{\"P1-P3\": 0.5888, \"P1-R1\": 0.4953, \"P3-R1\": 0.533}`.\n- Adjudication: 1618 of 5084 stage-1 items\n  (0.3183) went to P1 across 538 sentences. P1 agreement counts:\n  `{\"P1_agrees_P3\": 928, \"n_P3\": 1428, \"P1_agrees_R1\": 374, \"n_R1\": 684}`. Disguise (plan 5a)\n- Each sentence gets its own CVCVC nonce bijection. It covers the Porter stems of the text's content words and the name\n  tokens of ALL formulas of that sentence.\n  - It is seeded by sha1(sentence_id), and a nonce is rejected if it is a WordNet lemma.\n  - A name token that is a WordNet synonym of a sentence word reuses that word's nonce.\n  - Logic symbols, quantifiers and numbers are unchanged.\n- Guard: plain-z3 equivalence of class representatives before vs after disguise, 50 sentences:\n  `{\"sentences\": 50, \"pairs\": 957, \"mismatches\": 0, \"z3_unknown_pairs\": 0, \"passed\": true}`.\n- Deviation from the plan: NLTK Porter stems are used instead of spaCy lemmas.\n- `metadata_disguised_text` and `metadata_disguised_fol` are on every row, for contamination checks.\n\n## 10. Disjointness and exclusion\n- Exclusion hashes: 2054. They cover FOLIO-validation (tasksource and refined), the curated\n  FOLIO/MALLS sets, MALLS-test, the Logic-LM FOLIO_dev contexts and the few-shot exemplars: `{\"tasksource_folio_validation\": 2634, \"folio_refined_validation\": 2634, \"curated_folio\": 859, \"curated_malls\": 200, \"malls_test\": 2000, \"logiclm_context\": 3876, \"logiclm_question\": 1224}`.\n- Collisions after exclusion: **0**; top-up collisions: 0.\n- MALLS pool log: `{\"raw\": 27284, \"gold_unparseable\": 334, \"excluded_screen_hash\": 1, \"dup_text\": 30, \"pool\": 26919}`. They are excluded from CTRL and calibration.\n\n## 11. Screen audit (secondary deliverable)\n- Logic-LM records per file: {'gpt-3.5-turbo': 204, 'gpt-4': 204, 'text-davinci-003': 204}. Curated conclusions matched:\n  112/202 (0.554).\n  Items per track: {'L': 871, 'H': 302}.\n- Panel scope (plan 6b): every track-L sentence with a VOCAB_GRAN/COMPOUND/TIMEOUT class, plus a 20% sha1 sample of the rest,\n  plus a supplementary pass over the VOCAB_GRAN strings themselves. The self-preference check\n  there must account for this; it does not affect these labels. The panel families (Anthropic, Zhipu, Moonshot) are\n  disjoint from all generators.\n- Known biases:\n  1. Tier-B/C labels carry panel error: about 0.73 accuracy on real errors, and the panel is strict (section 7).\n  2. A MALLS reference judged faithful may still be wrong if 2 of the 3 panel members missed the error.\n  3. CTRL references are FOLIO annotations the refiner left unchanged (mostly IDENTICAL_STRING); they are not independent\n     re-annotations.\n  4. Tier A depends on the iter-3 aligner. Its VOCAB/GRAN bridge can accept meaning-changing renames, which is why\n     VOCAB_GRAN rows go to the panel.\n  5. One prompt per generator at temperature 0, so system-level rankings are descriptive only.\n  6. The L25 top-up batch has only 5 generator slots.\n  7. It takes a sentence and a\ncandidate first-order-logic formula, with no gold available, and scores how likely the formula is unfaithful using every\nbaseline the user asked for:\n- the parse/compile rate;\n- the user's own pilot structural metrics, adapted to FOL;\n- round-trip scores (FOL→NL→compare), via NLI, embedding similarity, and re-formalisation checked with z3;\n- LLM-as-judge: two cheap judges plus a larger judge, each scored on the ORIGINAL and on a nonce-DISGUISED version of\n  the item (a contamination control).\n\nEverything is measured on the same frozen, labelled screen that experiments A, B and C use (joined by `item_id`, with\nlabel-vector sha1 in `data/label_vector.sha1`). It produces the **bar** that iteration-2 candidates must beat:\n`judge_cheap_disg` and the cross-fitted `best_baseline_oof`.\n\n> **Results:** `results/summary.md` has the plain-language tables. `results/analysis.json` has every number.\n> `method_out.json` / `full_method_out.json` have the per-item scores (exp_gen_sol_out schema, validated).\n\n## Key results (final: planned API judges)\nAll CIs are sentence-clustered bootstrap intervals (2,000 resamples). Track L means Logic-LM outputs labelled CORRECT\nvs ERROR, n = 524 (230 errors). Judges:\n- `judge_cheap`: gemini-2.5-flash-lite, rubric A.\n- `judge_cheap2`: gpt-4.1-nano, P(YES).\n- `judge_strong`: gemini-3.1-pro-preview on the pre-registered 200L + 96H subset.\n\nOpen-weight local judges (Qwen3-8B, Llama-3.1-8B, Qwen3-14B-nf4) are reported next to them as `judge_local_*`.\n\n**The screen.**\n- 1,090 items: track L 796 (3 Logic-LM systems; 108/202 curated conclusions and 128 agreed premises matched) and\n  track H 294.\n- Track-L labels: 294 CORRECT, 230 ERROR, 216 UNCERTAIN, 56 UNPARSEABLE.\n- 128 of the 230 track-L ERRORs (56%) are \"substitution-only\" (ADD+DROP of an unaligned predicate), i.e. likely\n  correct-but-not-equivalent.\n- On track H, 27% of the parseable original public gold is wrong, 5.8% of it is unparseable, and 22% of the\n  non-equivalent originals are VOCAB/GRAN.\n\n**Track-L AUROC [95% CI]:**\n\n| Group | Metric | AUROC [95% CI] |\n|---|---|---|\n| Cheap API judges | **judge_cheap_disg (the pre-registered bar)** | **0.777 [0.720, 0.827]** |\n| | judge_cheap_orig | 0.749 [0.695, 0.801] |\n| | judge_cheap2 (orig / disg) | 0.750 / 0.714 |\n| Frontier judge (200-item subset) | judge_strong_orig | **0.802 [0.715, 0.877]** |\n| | judge_strong_disg | 0.730 [0.655, 0.803] |\n| Local judges | Qwen3-8B (orig / disg) | 0.755 / 0.757 |\n| | Llama-3.1-8B (orig / disg) | 0.699 / 0.671 |\n| | Qwen3-14B-nf4 (orig / disg) | 0.753 / 0.760 |\n| Round-trip (API verbaliser) | NLI min | 0.710 [0.648, 0.769] |\n| | alt NLI checkpoint | 0.689 |\n| | mpnet cosine | 0.633 |\n| | re-formalise + z3 | 0.513 (near chance: the literal verbalisation is almost always re-formalisable) |\n| Round-trip (local verbaliser) | NLI min | 0.740 |\n| User's pilot metrics | joint-load conflict, arity, shape, dangling, undeclared | 0.50–0.53: **a negative result** |\n| | cross-system rerun Jaccard | 0.720 [0.659, 0.780] |\n| Parse rate | parse_fail | 0.598 on the view that counts UNPARSEABLE as error |\n\n**The frontier judge beats the cheap judge on the same items.** On the 200-item subset, the original-text frontier judge\nscores 0.802 vs 0.725 for the cheap judge: Δ = +0.077 [0.006, 0.154], DeLong p = 0.015. Disguised, the gap\ndisappears.\n\n**Cross-fitted combination of all cheap baselines (S4).** OOF AUROC is 0.817 [0.762, 0.867], which is only +0.039\n[−0.010, 0.087] over the bar: not significant. The bar for iteration 2 is therefore `judge_cheap_disg` **0.777** and\n`best_baseline_oof` **0.817**, with folds in `data/folds.json`.\n\n**Contamination.** No evidence.\n- Every DiD CI includes 0: cheap judge −0.042 / −0.053, nano −0.077 / −0.065, frontier +0.002.\n- The frontier judge loses about 0.07 AUROC under disguise on BOTH tracks, i.e. disguise removes lexical meaning; it\n  does not expose memorised gold.\n- In the recall probe, flash-lite reproduces the erroneous ORIGINAL public gold on 4.4% of the 113 items where original\n  and corrected differ, vs 15.9% for the CORRECTED gold.\n- Its predicate-name overlap with the original gold does not exceed its overlap with an independent LLM translation:\n  Δ = −0.068 [−0.171, 0.034].\n\n**Invariance.** False-alarm rates on meaning-preserving rewrites of CORRECT items, FA base → FA rewrite:\n\n| Metric | Contrapositive | De Morgan | Rename |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| judge_cheap_orig | 0.03→0.67 | 0.05→0.18 | 0.04→0.27 |\n| judge_cheap_disg | 0.10→0.51 | 0.10→0.56 | 0.13→0.55 |\n| gpt-4.1-nano | 0.03→0.97 | — | 0.19→0.65 |\n| rt_reformalise_eq | 0.08→0.03 | — | 0.11→0.23 |\n\n- REORDER is almost free for every metric.\n- rt_reformalise_eq is the most invariant metric (overall 0.10→0.15), but it does not discriminate.\n- The pilot structural metrics are rename-fragile by construction.\n\n**Complexity.**\n- Track L is dominated by short sentences (485/524 have fewer than 12 words), so no long, heavily conditioned stratum\n  is testable on this screen.\n- The disguised judge's correctness falls with sentence length on track L: logit slope per SD of words = −0.48,\n  p = 1.6e-5.\n- Track-H cells are descriptive only (n ≤ 41).\n\n**Error types.** The judge's error-type labels are rarely right: track H 0/12 on single-op errors, track L 18/89.\n\n**Label noise.** 158 track-L ERRORs are judged faithful by judge_cheap (84 of them substitution-only). They are exported\nin `results/judge_label_disagreements.csv` for adjudication by dataset E.\n\n**Cost per call (measured):**\n\n| Judge / step | $ per call |\n|---|---|\n| flash-lite judge | $0.00004 |\n| gpt-4.1-nano | $0.000014 |\n| frontier judge | $0.0029 |\n| verbalise / re-formalise | about $0.00003 |\n\nTotal API spend: **$2.02** (cap $9.5).\n\n**Independent audit** (`tests/audit_headlines.py` → `results/audit_headlines.json`). This is a separate code path: raw\nscore files, explicit pairwise Mann-Whitney AUROC, a python-`random` cluster bootstrap, and an S4 re-fit with its own\nsklearn Pipeline.\n\n| Quantity | Original | Audit |\n|---|---|---|\n| judge_cheap_disg AUROC | 0.777 | 0.777 (0.785 if the 11 JSON failures are dropped rather than flagged) |\n| rt_nli_min AUROC | 0.710 | 0.710 |\n| pilot_rerun_jacc AUROC | 0.720 | 0.720 |\n| S4 combination − judge | +0.039 | +0.038 [−0.010, 0.086] |\n| frontier − cheap | +0.077 | +0.077 [0.008, 0.153] |\n| DiD | −0.053 | −0.053 [−0.145, 0.039] |\n\nPlacebos (shuffled labels):\n- Every AUROC is in 0.46–0.54.\n- S4 OOF AUROC is 0.481.\n- The frontier − cheap Δ CI includes 0.\n- The placebo DiD CI includes 0, and the placebo DiD is the same size as the real one.\n\n## Deviation 1: local open-weight judges during the key outage, then planned API judges\nThe run's shared OpenRouter key hit its $50/day limit at 13:29 UTC, before any sweep. Every LLM step was first run on\nthe local GPU (RTX 4000 Ada) with open-weight models, the same prompts and T=0:\n- Qwen3-8B JSON judge;\n- Llama-3.1-8B P(YES);\n- Qwen3-14B-nf4;\n- Qwen3-8B verbaliser and recall probe.\n\nThese are pre-registered in `prereg_local.json` (frozen 13:49 UTC) and stored under `results/scores/*_local*.jsonl`,\n`results/promptdev_local.json` and `results/roundtrip_meta_local.json`.\n\nWhen a fresh key arrived (15:11 UTC), the **planned API judges were run** under `prereg.json` (frozen 15:13 UTC):\n- The rubric variant (A) comes from the pre-outage API dev-slice run (`results/promptdev_api_preoutage.json`).\n- The frontier judge was chosen by a cost/validity-only 10-item dry run: gemini-3.1-pro-preview at $0.0033/call beat\n  gpt-5 (low) at $0.0036 and gemini-2.5-pro at $0.0083, all 10/10 valid. gemini-3-pro-preview is no longer listed.\n- The strong subsample is identical to the local prereg.\n\nThe local judges' track-L results had been seen before this prereg, but no API setting was tuned on track L\n(`freeze_context` in `prereg.json`).\n\nTwo budget notes:\n- A budget-guard bug made queued coroutines pre-reserve budget, so the first frontier sweep refused 263 calls. It was\n  fixed (`src/budget.py`: reservation inside the semaphore), and the sweep was completed from the cache.\n- Parallel stages raced on `budget_state.json`; it was reconciled from the append-only `costs.jsonl`.\n\n## Other departures from the plan text (all logged)\n- **Labeller determinism.** The iter-3 repair search used a 25 s wall-clock budget, which makes labels depend on machine\n  load. `src/labeller/labeller.py:search_det` uses a deterministic candidate budget (60k level-2 candidates) and\n  `PYTHONHASHSEED=0`, because the fingerprint uses `hash()`. Regression against iter-3 `repair_census.rows.json`: 96/99\n  unchanged, and exactly the 3 ⊕/→-bracketing COMPOUNDs become EQUIV (`results/labeller_regression.json`). T1 unit tests\n  pass.\n- **Disguise.** The formula is disguised by token-level substitution of identifiers, so bracketing and whitespace are\n  byte-identical between the orig and disg conditions. It is verified on the AST: the inverse map gives an identical AST\n  and the arity multiset is unchanged. Lemmas are keyed by their Porter stem, so \"engaged\"/\"Engaged\" share one nonce.\n  0/1,090 items fail an assertion, and the formula leak rate is 0 (`results/disguise_audit.txt`,\n  `results/disguise_stats.json`).\n- **Verbalisation cleaning.** The local verbaliser sometimes answers `The formula ¬(…) says: \"…\"`. A deterministic\n  cleaner (`judges.clean_verbalisation`) keeps only the English sentence, so the formula cannot leak into NLI or\n  re-formalisation.\n- **Track H labels.** The plan's literal mapping sends COMPOUND to UNCERTAIN; this is `label`, the shared label vector.\n  A second view, `label_h_curator`, counts COMPOUND as ERROR, because curators certified the original was wrong. It is\n  used for per-type sensitivity and reported as `H_curator_view`.\n- **Extra sensitivity set** `L_exclude_subst_only`. ERROR items whose only repair is ADD+DROP of an unaligned predicate\n  (e.g. `EatSalads(taylor)` vs `RegularlyEat(taylor, salad)`) are mostly vocabulary mismatches the aligner missed.\n- **Extra metrics:**\n  - `pilot_shape_incons`, from the pilot's metric2 argument-shape check;\n  - `rt_nli_contra`;\n  - `rt_nli_min_alt`, the alt NLI checkpoint;\n  - `REPRINT`, a formatting-only rewrite control.\n- `HF_HOME` stays on the run's shared cache (harness rule), not `./hf_cache`.\n\n## Layout\n| Path | What |\n|---|---|\n| `method.py` | Orchestrator; every step is a stage (`--stage NAME [--limit N]`), see its docstring |\n| `src/labeller/fol.py` | iter-3 FOL parser/z3 with the ⊕ precedence fix (`PREC['xor']=3`) |\n| `src/labeller/repair_census.py` | iter-3 aligner, granularity bridge and typed-repair operators (unchanged except the import) |\n| `src/labeller/labeller.py` | `equivalent_modulo_vocab(cand, ref)`: the shared labeller; strata helpers |\n| `src/screen.py` | Shared frozen screen: Logic-LM parsing, sentence matching, agreed premises, labelling, strata, `item_id` |\n| `src/disguise.py` | Nonce disguise of text and formula, with assertions (i)-(v) |\n| `src/rewrites.py` | Meaning-preserving rewrites (RENAME/REORDER/DEMORGAN/PRENEX/CONTRAPOSITIVE/REPRINT), z3-verified |\n| `src/judges.py` | Prompts (rubric A/B, JSON, YES/NO, verbalise, re-formalise, recall), parsing, and the API judge path |\n| `src/local_llm.py` | Local GPU backend: batched greedy generation, P(YES) scoring, cache |\n| `src/budget.py` | Budget-guarded async OpenRouter client (per-component caps, cost ledger) |\n| `src/roundtrip.py` | NLI (both directions, two checkpoints) and mpnet cosine |\n| `src/pilot_metrics.py` | The user's pilot metrics adapted to FOL (see `results/pilot_mapping.md`) |\n| `src/analysis.py`, `src/report.py` | Meta-evaluation: AUROC/AUPRC, cluster bootstrap, DeLong, contamination, combination, per-type, invariance, complexity, coverage, cost |\n| `tests/test_labeller.py` | T1 unit tests |\n| `tests/audit_headlines.py` | Independent re-derivation of the headline numbers, with shuffled-label placebos |\n| `prereg.json`, `prereg.sha1` | API-judge pre-registration (frozen before any API score was joined to a label; see `freeze_context`) |\n| `prereg_local.json`, `prereg_local.sha1` | Local-judge pre-registration (frozen 13:49 UTC, before any local score was joined) |\n| `data/screen_items.json` | **The meta-evaluation dataset**: 1,090 items (track L 796, track H 294) with labels, repair ops, strata and story context |\n| `data/label_vector.sha1` | sha1 of the sorted `item_id:label` lines (identity check against A/B/C) |\n| `data/invariance_items.json` | 282 z3-verified meaning-preserving rewrites of 150 track-L CORRECT items |\n| `data/disguise_map.json` | Per item (and rewrite): disguised text/formula, lemma→nonce map, audit flags |\n| `data/folds.json` | GroupKFold fold per track-L CORRECT/ERROR item (reuse for nested ΔAUROC in iteration 2) |\n| `data/logiclm/` | Logic-LM released `FOLIO_dev_{gpt-3.5-turbo,gpt-4,text-davinci-003}.json` |\n| `data/screen_build_log.json` | Match rates, premise agreement, label distributions, parse stats |\n| `results/scores/*.jsonl` | Raw per-unit scores (judges orig/disg, round-trip, pilot, recall probe) |\n| `results/analysis.json` | Every table: sets × metrics, paired deltas, contamination, combination, per-type, invariance, complexity, system level, coverage, cost, label quality |\n| `method_out.json`, `full_method_out.json`, `mini_method_out.json`, `preview_method_out.json` | exp_gen_sol_out per-item output at the workspace root (full = identical copy; mini = 3 examples per dataset; preview = mini with strings truncated); `uv run method.py` (default stage `analysis`) regenerates them from the cached scores |\n| `results/method_out.json` | Same content, next to the other results |\n| `results/tables/*.csv` | CSV versions of the main tables |\n| `results/judge_label_disagreements.csv` | Track-L items where the judge disagrees with the auto label (for adjudication in iteration 2) |\n| `results/per_item_scores.jsonl`, `results/rewrite_scores.jsonl` | Flat per-item / per-rewrite scores |\n| `results/local_cache.jsonl`, `results/llm_cache.jsonl` | Every LLM input→output (local and API), for exact re-runs |\n| `results/summary.md` | Plain-language results |\n| `logs/` | Stage logs |\n\n## How to run\n```bash\nbash restore.sh all                      # venv, NLTK corpora, pilot unzip, model weights into the shared HF cache\nexport PYTHONHASHSEED=0\nfor s in labeller screen prep pilot; do .venv/bin/python method.py --stage $s; done\n# planned API path (OpenRouter; ≈$2; every call cached in results/llm_cache.jsonl)\nfor s in promptdev prereg; do .venv/bin/python method.py --stage $s; done\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage judges --limit 20 && .venv/bin/python method.py --stage judges\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage strong --limit 20 && .venv/bin/python method.py --stage strong\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage llm_api            # API verbalise/re-formalise + recall probe\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage rt_equiv && .venv/bin/python method.py --stage roundtrip   # z3 + GPU NLI/embeddings\n# secondary local-GPU judges (open weights; ≈75 min on an RTX 4000 Ada; cached in results/local_cache.jsonl)\nfor s in promptdev_local prereg_local; do .venv/bin/python method.py --stage $s; done\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage llm_local && .venv/bin/python method.py --stage rt_llm_local\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage rt_equiv --variant local && .venv/bin/python method.py --stage roundtrip --variant local\n.venv/bin/python method.py --stage analysis\n.venv/bin/python tests/audit_headlines.py              # independent re-derivation + placebos\n```\n\n## Restoring removed files\n| Deleted path (see `.aii/manifest.yaml`) | Restore |\n|---|---|\n| `.venv/` | `bash restore.sh venv` (uv venv + `requirements.txt` + `python -m spacy download en_core_web_sm`) |\n| `pilot_ref/` | `bash restore.sh pilot` (unzips `user_uploads/dpv_pilot_study.zip`) |\n| `src/**/__pycache__/` | regenerated automatically |\n| model weights (not in this workspace; they live in the shared HF cache) | `bash restore.sh models` |\n", "numLines": 245, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 245}}

{"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_strat/gen_strat_1/.terminal_claude_agent_struct_out.json", "content": "{\n  \"strategies\": [\n    {\n      \"domain_reasoning\": \"FIELD: meta-evaluation of reference-free (gold-free) quality metrics, applied to neuro-symbolic text-to-logic (NL->FOL autoformalization). Its norms come from three places: semantic-parsing and autoformalization evaluation (neurosymbolic handbook), summarization-factuality meta-evaluation (TRUE, Honovich et al. NAACL 2022; SummaC; FRANK; Goyal & Durrett 2021), and the WMT metrics shared tasks (Freitag et al.; Deutsch, Dror & Roth TACL 2021 on bootstrap CIs for metric correlations; Deutsch et al. EMNLP 2023 'Ties Matter'). What I read for this step: the neurosymbolic handbook; iteration 1's four artifacts (per-item scores, READMEs, dataset card §3-§11); and the reviewer's audit scripts (review_report/audit: paired_boot, rescore_adjudicated, join_audit). I did no new web lookups, so the MT and summarization norms below are cited from standing knowledge and are treated as provisional where they go beyond the handbook. (1) PRINCIPLES. A metric is only as credible as its labels. The handbook's hard rule is never to score on FOLIO/MALLS as shipped (about 36-39% of gold is wrong, 2606.02837). Compiling or being provable is not faithfulness (2604.19459, 2606.16541). Gold-free round-trip certification is an occupied lane (2604.25031), so it can only be a baseline. Still disputed: whether any gold-free faithfulness number can be accepted as primary (handbook open question 3), and whether LLM judges can be trusted on public benchmarks they may have memorised. What must hold before anyone reads a result: the labels are named, the labeller does not share an instrument with the metric, and unparseable outputs stay in the denominator. (2) WHAT CONVINCES. Item-level, threshold-free discrimination (ROC AUC) on REAL system outputs, compared PAIRED on the same items against the obvious baseline. The weights and threshold are frozen on one set and scored once on a disjoint held-out set. The result must stay stable when the label source changes. In MT metrics a win on one human-label protocol (MQM vs DA) is not believed until it holds on the other, and the same logic applies here. The panel labels and the solver labels reversed our iteration-1 ranking, so label-regime robustness IS the evidence standard for this claim. System-level correlation is secondary and must carry CIs. With few systems it is descriptive only. (3) STANDARD MOVES and what each rules out: - a paired, cluster (sentence) bootstrap: item difficulty and within-sentence dependence; - a held-out confirmation set scored once with frozen weights: winner's curse and threshold tuning on the test set; - nonce disguise: memorised-gold contamination; - meaning-preserving rewrite suites: a metric that tracks surface form; - strata by length and conditions: 'works only on short sentences'; - a family-disjoint adjudicator gated on expert labels: label noise passing as metric error; - an instrument-disjointness check between labeller and metric: circular validation; - nested cross-fitted ΔAUROC over the best baseline COMBINATION: 'your metric is just a re-weighting of what we have'; - tie-aware comparison for quantized metrics (Ties Matter). (4) USUAL FAILURE MODES. (a) Shared-instrument circularity. Our c_score shares align() with the solver labeller, and L2-bow shares lexical sensitivity with the panel; MT has the same problem when metrics are trained on the very human-judgment protocol they are scored against. (b) Comparing numbers from different label vectors. Iteration 1 did this with three vectors and 588 shared ids. (c) Synthetic perturbations standing in for real errors. (d) Tuning on the confirmation set, or subgroup hunting after a failure. (e) An LLM adjudicator from the same family as a metric or generator (self-preference). Gemini and Qwen are both generators and judge families in dataset E. (f) Underpowered long strata reported anyway. (g) Precision quoted without its prevalence. (h) Correct-but-not-equivalent outputs counted as errors, or, as the audit showed, 'vocabulary-aligned = CORRECT' when 58 of 59 label transitions went CORRECT->ERROR.\",\n      \"principle_alignment\": \"FOLLOWS. (1) Screen/confirmation separation. PEER+TEXT features, the fusion weights and the threshold are fitted only on the iteration-1 screen (track L/H) and written, with a sha256, into prereg.json BEFORE any dataset-E label column is loaded. E is then scored once, with no subgroup hunting. (2) Paired comparison on identical ids. Every headline Δ is computed on the same E item_ids, with a sentence-clustered bootstrap (sentence_id). Label vectors are hashed and checked across artifacts, fixing iteration 1's three-vector mistake. (3) Label-regime robustness is part of the claim, not a footnote. The regimes are R_AB (panel tiers A+B), R_A (solver tier A), R_ADJ (a new gated reference-aware adjudicator) and R_COMP (references trusted by construction). The regime-shift table is a deliverable. (4) Instrument disjointness is enforced by design. Name-free alignment (F1) removes c_score's align() dependency on the labeller. Fusion weights are fitted only on screen items where the solver and panel labels AGREE, so neither confound sets the weights. (5) Contamination. Disguised versions are run for every LLM component, and the minimum detectable DiD is stated. (6) Invariance. ONE shared rewrite file (exp D data/invariance_items.json, 282 rewrites) is used for every metric, so P4 compares like with like. (7) Coverage. Unparseable rows stay in the denominator in a pre-registered coverage view. (8) Nested ΔAUROC over S4 (all cheap baselines + judge), not just over the judge. DELIBERATELY BROKEN. (a) Full confirmation needs all four regimes in one artifact, but R_ADJ and R_COMP are built in parallel this iteration and cannot be consumed yet. So this iteration confirms under R_AB and R_A, and R_ADJ/R_COMP scoring happens in iteration 3 by an item_id join. This stays credible because every PEER+TEXT score on E is written to disk with the prereg hash before those labels exist, and the adjudicator never sees any metric score. (b) The headline comparator (disguised cheap judge) is run twice: in the PEER+TEXT experiment, so headline (a) is computed in-iteration, and in the baseline experiment, for S4. The duplication is deliberate: it costs about $0.4 and yields a judge test-retest reliability number that the paper needs anyway. (c) R_COMP is semi-synthetic, which breaks 'real errors only'. The candidates are still REAL outputs of the same 10 generator slots, only the sentence is composed. It is a separate stratum, never pooled, and exists only because L25 tier A is untestable (the panel rejects 85% of references there). (d) Fitting on label-agreeing screen items selects easier items. A pre-registered sensitivity variant fitted on ALL screen items (adjudicated labels) is also frozen and reported, never promoted. (e) The adjudicator is an LLM, which risks circularity with the LLM metrics. It comes from a family that is neither generator nor metric judge. It is gated on 173 expert or known-label items, split into a dev half used for at most 2 prompt variants and a gate half. A second, fully family-disjoint adjudicator (xAI Grok-class) checks family independence on the calibration set.\",\n      \"title\": \"Cheap translators plus text checks vs judges\",\n      \"objective\": \"Establish PEER+TEXT as a gold-free NL->FOL faithfulness metric. PEER+TEXT is name-free, solver-graded clause consensus across cheap cross-family translators, fused with solver-exact text accounting. The target is a metric that beats the disguised cheap LLM judge (and adds over the best combination of all requested baselines) on HELD-OUT real outputs, under several independent label regimes. It should stay rewrite-invariant where judges flip, and name the error type, especially the dropped condition, on long, heavily conditioned sentences.\",\n      \"rationale\": \"LATCH / DEEPEN. The object is fixed: iteration 1's two leads. Cross-family consensus reached 0.866 on solver labels but only 0.776 on panel labels. FOL-Triage's text layers reached 0.759 on solver labels but 0.872 on panel labels. Each beat the disguised cheap judge (0.777) under exactly ONE label regime. Those opposite dependencies are the diagnosis, and each lead has an identified shared-instrument confound. The mechanism for fusing them is already measured: peers miss 38% of errors, and those are the errors the medoid endorses (COVERAGE 41%, STRUCT 57%, POLARITY 5%). Text accounting sees shared and rename errors, and consensus sees family-specific structure errors. So all five slots attack the one object, PEER+TEXT, from different sides. FIX + CONFIRM (exp 1): F1 name-free alignment, F2 graded clause consensus and F3 label-agreeing calibration, frozen on the screen and scored once on held-out E. FAIR BAR (exp 2): every requested baseline, plus S4, re-run on E with the same ids, and candidate B2 executed once. CLEANER LABELS (dataset R_ADJ): a gated reference-aware adjudicator. This answers the confound objection a reviewer names first. BOUNDARY / REPLICATION where the user cares most (dataset R_COMP): long, heavily conditioned sentences whose references are trusted by construction, plus a typed-perturbation suite. MECHANISM PRE-TEST + MUST-FIX RECORD (evaluation): complementarity (P1/P2) on the screen with existing per-item scores, and the reviewer's blocking corrections computed from artifacts, not prose. Closed strands get no slot: L1 on LLM outputs, L2-role, round-trip re-formalisation, and the pilot metrics except as a free baseline column. SHARED DEFINITIONS (every artifact implements them verbatim). [E-POOL] Dataset E heldout_candidates, LLM systems only; malls_gpt4_gold and ccg2lambda rows are scored but reported separately. R_AB = label_tier in {A, B}, final_label in {CORRECT, ERROR}, excluding CONTESTED, reading_choice and UNRESOLVED. R_A = tier A only. It is testable only where the dataset card's §3 says so; everywhere else it is sign-only. Pooled L20+EXC+CTRL tier A is the R_A headline. PRIMARY = parseable rows. COVERAGE VIEW = UNPARSEABLE rows added as ERROR, with each metric's pre-declared fallback score (PEER+TEXT: p=1). Bootstrap: 2,000 resamples clustered by metadata sentence_id, seed 0. Strata: source_stratum L25/L20/EXC/CTRL, and words/n_quant/depth/n_conditions/exception_type from metadata. Untestable cells (<50/50) are declared before scoring. [SCREEN] Exp A's screen_items.json/href_items.json item_ids. Labels are exp A's solver labels, plus dataset E's screen_adjudicated_labels.json (1,173 rows). 'Label-agreeing' = solver final label == adjudicated final_label, tiers A/B/auto_only. [REWRITES] Exp D data/invariance_items.json (282 rewrites: RENAME 150, REORDER 41, DEMORGAN 39, CONTRAPOSITIVE 39, REPRINT 12, PRENEX 1). The judges' scores on it already exist in exp D results/rewrite_scores.jsonl, and every new metric scores exactly these rw_ids. [ADJ-PROMPT] The adjudicator sees the ORIGINAL sentence, the candidate FOL, and a reference FOL tagged VERIFIED or UNVERIFIED. It is told that predicate names and decomposition may legitimately differ and that it must judge meaning only. It returns JSON: {verdict: FAITHFUL | UNFAITHFUL | AMBIGUOUS_READING, ops: subset of [NEG, REV, QUANT, RESTR, CONN, MOVE, DROP, ADD, SWAP, BIND, SCOPE, UNGLUE, MEANING_RENAME, OTHER], location: short span of the sentence, reference_wrong: bool}. Settings: temperature 0, no reasoning trace requested, and one fixed prompt text released as adjudication_prompt.txt. Dataset R_COMP copies the same spec verbatim. POSITIVE TARGET. The paper's lead sentence if it holds: 'agreement among cheap translators, graded clause-by-clause by a solver with name-free alignment, fused with solver-exact text accounting, beats a disguised LLM judge on held-out real outputs under both panel and solver labels, and stays rewrite-invariant where judges flip.' If only one regime holds, the regime-shift table becomes the finding. That is still a positive methodological result, because it quantifies how much label choice alone moves metric rankings. The per-type crossover (P1) tells the user which cheap signal owns which error mass, whatever the headline.\",\n      \"artifact_directions\": [\n        {\n          \"type\": \"experiment\",\n          \"objective\": \"Build PEER+TEXT with the three component fixes (F1 name-free alignment, F2 graded clause consensus, F3 label-agreeing calibration). F1 name_free_align(cand_fol, peer_fol, text=None). Build a signature per predicate and constant: arity; z3 monotonicity polarity of each occurrence (DOWN/UP/MIXED); quantifier block index and force; argument-sharing graph (which variables and slots it co-occurs with); and an optional text anchor (the L2 stem/WordNet span it matches in the sentence). Solve an assignment (Hungarian on signature distance, then the top-k ≤5 alternative assignments). ACCEPT the mapping that maximises the number of verified clause entailments under it. Names never enter the cost except through the optional anchor term. Pre-register two variants: NF-pure (no anchor) and NF-anchored (the anchor as a soft cost). Select ONE on the screen by this rule: highest screen AUROC among variants with RENAME FA ≤0.10 on the shared rewrite set. Over-alignment is measured by a MEANING_RENAME probe: on screen CORRECT items, replace one predicate by a different same-arity predicate from the same story, 150 items, sha1-seeded. Report recall on that probe and on SWAP. F2 graded_consensus(text, fol, peers) -> {g_score, support, coverage, unit_codes, n_unknown}. Claim units are top-level conjuncts after NNF/prenex normalisation, each universal rule split as (restrictor set -> consequent), plus existential claims. support = the mean over candidate units of the fraction of peers entailing the unit under the NF map. coverage = the fraction of peer-majority units (supported by ≥50% of peers) that the candidate entails. g_score = 1 - F1(support, coverage). unit_codes: an unsupported unit is ADD; an uncovered majority unit is DROP (with its location); candidate entails the negation of a majority unit = NEG; match only after a ∀/∃ flip = QUANT; match only after argument permutation = SWAP. The medoid-repair operators (exp C) are kept as a second readout. Peers are leave-one-FAMILY-out. On E the peers are the other generator slots of the same sentence; Llama-70B and Qwen3 zero-shot rows are the same family as their few-shot slots. malls_gpt4_gold and ccg2lambda are never peers. On the screen the peers are exp C's 6 fresh peers plus the 2 other Logic-LM systems. A z3 timeout (2 s) makes the unit UNKNOWN, excluded from both means, and n_unknown is reported. Report g_score also with peers subsampled to k=6, a pre-registered pool-size sensitivity check, since the screen and E pools differ. Also compute c_score_nf (exact equivalence under the NF map) and c_score_align (the iteration-1 method, as a reference column). TEXT SIDE: content_accounting (L2-bow) and role_questionnaire + l3_compare (L3-z3) from exp A's fol_triage.py, unchanged, using gemini-2.5-flash-lite with text only. On E that is 700 sentences plus their disguised versions, about $0.3. L1 and L2-role are emitted as columns only (closed strands, no tuning). F3 FUSION peer_text_score(text, fol, peers): logistic regression on [g_score, c_score_nf, L2-bow, L3-z3], standardised with screen statistics. Fit it on screen L+H items where the solver label and the adjudicated label AGREE, with 5 sentence-grouped folds to pick C from {0.1, 1, 10}. The final fit uses all agreeing items. Set the threshold to FA = 0.10 on screen CORRECT items from LLM systems only. The frozen sensitivity variant is fitted on ALL screen items with adjudicated labels. Also freeze single-signal PEER (g_score) and TEXT (L2-bow+L3 logistic) so P1/P2 can be run. COMPARATOR on E: judge_cheap_disg with exp D's exact rubric-A prompt and disguise code on all E LLM rows. Also run judge_cheap_orig on the same rows (~7.4k × 2 calls, ≈$0.8), and log $ per call. ANALYSIS (analyse_E.py, after the prereg check). (a) Paired Δ PEER+TEXT − judge_cheap_disg, pooled over E under R_AB and R_A, per stratum (L25/L20/EXC/CTRL), and in the long pool L25+L20+EXC. Report AUROC, AUPRC, and precision/recall/FA at the frozen threshold with the prevalence stated. (b) P1 crossover: per-type recall at matched FA (0.10 and 0.20) for PEER vs TEXT vs judge. Types come from E's tier-A repair_ops/error_ops: QUANT/SCOPE/BIND/SWAP/NEG vs ADD/DROP, plus peer-endorsed errors (candidate ≡_NF medoid). (c) P2: Spearman(PEER, TEXT) among true errors. Decompose the fusion's AUROC gain over the better single signal into the parts from peer-endorsed and non-endorsed errors. (d) P3: logistic interactions of score × z(words) for c_score_nf, g_score, fused and judge. Report fused-minus-judge Δ by stratum. (e) P4: FA and flip rate per rewrite family on the SHARED rewrite set for g_score, c_score_nf, L2-bow, L3 and fused, next to the judge rows from exp D rewrite_scores.jsonl. (f) Error-type readout accuracy: unit_codes and medoid ops vs tier-A repair_ops on 1-2-op errors, against chance and against the judge's type label. (g) System-level Kendall τ with bootstrap CI over the 13 system×variant rows, for fused and for the judge, against R_AB error rates. (h) Complexity curves (AUROC and FA vs words, n_quant, depth, n_conditions, exception_type). (i) Contamination: original vs disguised for L3 and the judge, paired, with the minimum detectable DiD stated. (j) Coverage (all failures in the denominator) and $ and seconds per item. Per-item output keyed by E item_id: all component scores, the fused p, fired signal, unit_codes, n_unknown and cost. This is what iteration 3 joins with R_ADJ and R_COMP. COMPUTE. z3 work runs in a ProcessPool on 7 vCPUs with a pair cache keyed by (sha1(cand), sha1(peer)). Scale mini (20 sentences) -> 100 -> all per aii-long-running-tasks. Per-type recall at matched FA (0.10/0.20) for c_score vs L2-bow vs L3 vs judge, with types from repair_ops and medoid-endorsed status. Spearman(c_score, text score) among true errors. Decompose the fusion gain into peer-endorsed vs non-endorsed errors. (5) MUST-FIX tables transcribed with source paths: - exp D: H_primary, H_curator_view, L_exclude_subst_only, L_unparseable_as_error (parse_fail 0.598), L_borderline_to_uncertain, L_pessimistic, the full invariance table with base→rewrite FA and REPRINT, the paired Δ table with DeLong p, the H complexity table, coverage and cost per component, and the frontier same-item paired Δs (L +0.077 [0.006, 0.154], H +0.115 / +0.148); - exp A: the ERROR∪COMPOUND and minus-ADD+DROP sets, binary-layer TPR/FPR/precision at 10% and 25% prevalence, the L3 HREF field gate, fusion coefficients, test-retest, the qwen3-30b row, and the track-H paired AUROC; - exp C: lenient/vocab-strict/track H, the multi-family n=1,686 sample, cross-fitted [c_score, sc5], the RENAME breakdown, the 9-system Spearman with a bootstrap CI, and the medoid operator match (81% vs 46%; coarse 92% vs 78%); - dataset E: panel acceptance of expert-corrected formulas (0.613), 0.727 on real errors, 0.822 MALLS-gold rejection, the tier counts, the §4 gold-audit table, the §11 screen-audit cross-tab and the label-transition counts (58/62 CORRECT→ERROR vs 1/1). (6) Judge recall at MATCHED FA, orig vs disg, per type (the correction for 'reads more carefully'). Contamination minimum detectable DiD from the bootstrap SEs, and the significant single-track disguise drops (nano H −0.114, H_curator −0.102). (9) The coverage-against-request table (requirement → where answered → status) and the function inventory with a one-line definition per function (src/fol_triage.py, src/consensus.py, repair_census.py). Output eval_out.json and tables/*.csv, with a figure spec for the regime-shift matrix and the common-item forest plot.\",\n          \"what_it_would_show\": \"If it works, the paper gets a corrected, file-verified iteration-1 record. But the text-plus-consensus combination already beats the judge and S4 on the screen under adjudicated labels, and the two signals are nearly disjoint among errors (Spearman <0.4), with consensus owning structure errors and text owning add/drop. Released functions: name_free_align, graded_consensus, peer_text_score. (2) Every requested baseline is on the same E ids, with S4 OOF and fold ids, the frontier judge's same-item gain and its cost, the contamination MDEs, and B2's first real execution. (3) R_ADJ: a gated, family-independent, reference-aware adjudication of about 1,200 E rows plus the screen's disagreement items, with regime-transition tables and gold-error / correct-but-not-equivalent rates. (4) R_COMP: a powered long, conditioned, exception stratum with references trusted by construction, real outputs from 10 generators, and a typed-perturbation suite with MEANING_RENAME. Iteration 3 then needs only one join-and-score EVALUATION: criteria (a) under R_ADJ, (b) nested over S4 on identical folds, and (c) on R_COMP and the long strata, with PEER+TEXT (+B2 if gated) weights still frozen. The margin is larger in L25/L20/EXC than in CTRL. Two datasets add the label regimes that decide the claim: a gated reference-aware adjudicator, and a composed-long stratum with trusted-by-construction references plus typed perturbations. A text-only reader answers TRUE/FALSE/UNDETERMINED per world.\n- The reader is gated at balanced accuracy ≥0.85 on worlds from expert-corrected and verified references.\n- B2 runs on screen L+H first (a fusion feature for iteration 3), then on the shared rewrite set, then on all E LLM rows.\n\nANALYSES:\n- AUROC, AUPRC and precision at frozen thresholds, under R_AB, R_A and sensitivity regimes, pooled and per stratum;\n- paired Δ vs judge_cheap_disg (bootstrap + DeLong);\n- frontier vs cheap judge on identical rows, with the cost ratio against the $0.002 gate;\n- contamination DiD with minimum detectable DiD, including a direct gold-recognition probe on the malls_gpt4_gold rows;\n- judge recall per error type at matched FA;\n- complexity curves and a judge-correctness × length GEE;\n- coverage, $ and seconds per item;\n- gold-error and correct-but-not-equivalent rates;\n- system-level Kendall τ over 13 system×variant rows, with CIs.\n\nExpected spend: about $6.5, with a hard stop at $9.5.",

  "domain_practice": "WHAT I READ FOR THIS STEP:\n- the iteration-2 strategy, including its domain_reasoning, which cites the neurosymbolic handbook, TRUE, SummaC, FRANK, WMT and Deutsch et al.;\n- dataset E's dataset_card.md (§1-§13) and its preview rows;\n- exp D's README, prereg.json and src function inventory (judges, disguise, roundtrip, pilot_metrics, analysis, budget, labeller/repair_census.edits);\n- exp C's consensus.py (score_candidate, sc_scores);\n- the run memory for exp D (budget-guard bug, triton deregister, local verbaliser wrapper).\nThe 55-minute planning window did not allow new web lookups. The field norms below come from those sources and from standing knowledge of the metric meta-evaluation literature, and are flagged as such.\n\n(1) BASELINES EVERY PAPER OF THIS KIND REPORTS.\nReference-free faithfulness meta-evaluation always includes:\n- an LLM-as-judge (G-Eval-style rubric or yes/no probability);\n- an NLI-based consistency score (SummaC / TRUE's ANLI-T5 line);\n- a QA- or round-trip-based score (QAGS/QuestEval; for formal translation, back-translation plus NLI, as in the roundtrip verification line, arXiv 2604.25031);\n- sampling self-consistency (SelfCheckGPT-style; for FOL, semantic-equivalence clustering as in 2410.20936);\n- for code and logic, the trivial validity baseline (parse/compile rate).\nThe comparison a reviewer names first if missing is the LLM judge at a matched cost tier, plus a stronger judge showing what money buys.\nFair tuning of the judge means:\n- a fixed rubric chosen on a dev slice, never on the test set;\n- temperature 0;\n- a continuous score (probability or 0-100), not a binary verdict, because a binarised judge loses AUROC through ties (Deutsch et al. 'Ties Matter').\nThe expected combination baseline is a cross-fitted logistic stack of all cheap signals (the WMT/metric-ensemble convention). Without it a new metric can be 'just a re-weighting'.\n\n(2) DATA.\n- The handbook's rule: never score on FOLIO/MALLS gold as shipped. About 36-39% of it is wrong (2606.02837), and the dataset E panel rejects 82% of MALLS gold.\n- The accepted practice is real system outputs from several families with audited or adjudicated labels, not synthetic perturbations.\n- Dataset E follows it: 8,507 rows, 10 LLM slots over 9 families, and a blind, family-disjoint 3-model panel with tiers A/B/C.\n- Its known weaknesses: the panel is strict (accepts 0.613 of expert-corrected formulas); tier A is structurally rare on MALLS strata; L25 tier A is untestable.\n- FOLIO/MALLS are public, so memorisation is a live threat for any LLM component. The standard control is a nonce/entity-renaming disguise.\n\n(3) WHAT IS HELD CONSTANT.\n- Identical item ids and label vectors across the compared metrics. Iteration 1 broke this with three label vectors.\n- The same prompts, models, temperatures and disguise code as the screen-side bar (exp D), so the screen→E comparison is like-for-like.\n- The same sentence-clustered bootstrap (2,000 resamples, seed 0).\n- The same coverage convention: unparseable outputs stay in the denominator in a declared view.\n- The most likely confound: label source. The panel shares lexical sensitivity with LLM judges (both are LLMs reading names), so judges may be favoured under R_AB. The solver labeller shares align() with SC's equivalence modulo vocabulary, so SC may be favoured under solver tier A. Reporting every metric under both regimes, and exporting the feature table for R_ADJ, is how this field handles it (the WMT MQM-vs-DA analogue).\n\n(4) HOW MUCH IS ENOUGH.\n- Hanley-McNeil: AUROC SE ≈ 0.02 at about 300/300. The paired ΔAUROC MDE is about 0.04 pooled (E has about 860 CORRECT / 1,800 ERROR R_AB rows over about 600 sentences) and about 0.07-0.10 per stratum.\n- A stratum under 50 CORRECT / 50 ERROR over 25 sentences per side is untestable. Dataset E's §3 declaration already fixes this: L25/L20/EXC/CTRL are testable under A+B; tier A is testable only in L20 and EXC.\n- The frontier subsample of 300 gives a paired-Δ SE of about 0.035. It can detect the iteration-1 effect (+0.077) at roughly 80% power only if the effect holds, so it is reported with its CI and never as a null.\n- Kendall τ over 13 system rows has CIs of about ±0.4, so it is descriptive only, as the card states.\n\n(5) MEASURES AND CONVENTIONS.\n- ROC AUC (threshold-free) as primary, AUPRC with prevalence stated, and precision/recall/FA at a threshold frozen on another set.\n- Paired differences with a cluster bootstrap CI plus a DeLong p.\n- A tie rate reported for quantised metrics.\n- System-level Kendall τ-b with a bootstrap CI.\n- Cost in $ per item from the provider's usage.cost, and latency.\n- Contamination as a difference-in-differences with its minimum detectable effect, never as 'no evidence' alone.\n- Complexity reported as AUROC per bin plus a logistic interaction with cluster-robust SEs.",
  "practice_alignment": "MEETS:\n(a) Same-item paired comparison on one hashed label vector. prereg_baselines.json stores sha1 of the R_AB and R_A label vectors, computed by the same function the PEER+TEXT experiment uses, so iteration 3 can assert identity.\n(b) The judge is tuned fairly. Rubric A was selected on a screen dev slice in iteration 1 and is reused verbatim. T=0 and a continuous score.\n(c) A stronger judge on identical rows, with cost measured per call from usage.cost.\n(d) NLI round-trip and embedding round-trip, with two verbalisers and two NLI checkpoints.\n(e) SC-5 with the same model, T and scorer as iteration 1.\n(f) A cross-fitted stack (S4) on deterministic sentence-grouped folds shipped to iteration 3.\n(g) Cluster bootstrap by sentence_id, 2,000 resamples, and DeLong p.\n(h) The disguise control on every LLM component that reads a sentence or a formula, the MDE stated, and a direct gold-recognition probe using the malls_gpt4_gold rows.\n(i) Unparseable rows in a coverage view, with pre-declared fallback scores.\n(j) Strata untestable below 50/50, declared from the card before scoring.\n(k) Tie rates reported for the quantised metrics: SC-5 has 6 levels, the pilot metrics are binary, and B2 has at most 13 levels.\n\nDEPARTURES, with justification and cost:\n(1) The frontier judge runs on a 300-row stratified sample, not on all rows. The budget forces this ($0.003-0.004 per call × 2 conditions). Cost: the pooled Δ has a wide CI and there are no per-stratum frontier claims; that is stated in the tables. The sample is drawn from R_AB labels × stratum by a separate script before scoring and recorded in prereg, and an IPW-weighted AUROC is reported alongside.\n(2) The sampling frame for the frontier subsample reads labels. This is not tuning, because no parameter depends on it. It does break a strict 'no label before scoring' reading, so it is disclosed in prereg with the frame file's hash.\n(3) The pilot structural metrics need a 'document' that E rows do not have (they are single sentences). The MALLS document is an artificial (system, stratum, sha1-bucket of 20 sentences) group. This is logged as an adaptation. Cost: the joint-load conflict metric has a different meaning from the user's pilot, so it is reported as 'adapted'.\n(4) The rerun-Jaccard proxy uses zero-shot vs few-shot variants, which exist only for Llama-70B/Qwen3. For other slots it uses the cross-system Jaccard, as in exp D. Logged.\n(5) rt_reformalise_eq is not re-run: it was closed at 0.513 in iteration 1. It is dropped from S4, and S4 here is therefore not byte-identical to exp D's S4. The exact feature list is in prereg.\n(6) R_ADJ and R_COMP labels do not exist yet (they are built in parallel). This artifact scores under R_AB and R_A and exports everything iteration 3 needs to rescore without API calls: the per-item feature table, folds_E.json, fit_s4_oof(). That dependency is owned by the two parallel dataset artifacts and by the iteration-3 join evaluation.\n(7) B2 is a new metric with no literature norm. Its reader gate (≥0.85 balanced accuracy on known-truth worlds) follows the handbook's rule that a component must be validated before it is fused. Reader prompt dev is capped at 2 variants on a 40-item screen-H dev slice.\n(8) The judges are not run on the raw strings of unparseable rows. They get the pre-declared worst score, the same as PEER+TEXT's p=1. Cost: this slightly favours all metrics equally in the coverage view.\n(9) Judge recall 'at matched FA' uses per-judge thresholds set on E CORRECT rows. This is a descriptive analysis, not a deployable threshold, and it is labelled so. Deployable precision uses the thresholds frozen on the screen.\n(10) System-level τ over 13 rows is descriptive only.\nNo lookups beyond the local artifacts were made in planning. The executor must confirm model ids and prices via OpenRouter /models before any sweep.",
  "builds_on": "This is a DEEPEN iteration. Nothing is started fresh, and all code is reused from iteration 1. Read-only sources, copied into the workspace:\n\n(A) Dataset E (declared dependency art_U4Hsqt4Ay9Tg), /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/.\n- full_data_out is SPLIT: per its metadata.reading, concatenate each group's examples over sorted(glob('full_data_out/full_data_out_*.json')). If a single full_data_out.json exists, use it.\n- Groups used: heldout_candidates (8,507 rows, fields input JSON {text, candidate_fol, reference_fol, system, prompt_variant}, output = final label, metadata_item_id, metadata_sentence_id, metadata_system, metadata_slot, metadata_family, metadata_system_class, metadata_prompt_variant, metadata_label_tier, metadata_final_label, metadata_repair_ops, metadata_error_ops, metadata_correct_not_equivalent, metadata_reading_choice, metadata_reference_status, metadata_gold_audit_flag, metadata_strata{words, n_quant, depth, n_conditions, text_conditions, exception_type, source_stratum, ctrl_len_bin, l25_topup_batch}, metadata_disguised_text/fol, metadata_raw_output) and heldout_sentences.\n- Also screen_adjudicated_labels.json (only to key screen items for B2), dataset_card.md, and prompts/fewshot_v1.txt (the SC-5 prompt; see below).\n\n(B) Exp D, /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_4/. Copy src/ VERBATIM:\n- judges.py (judge_json, judge_logprob, simple_text, clean_verbalisation, parse_judge_json, p_yes_from_logprobs, gather_limited);\n- disguise.py (disguise_item);\n- roundtrip.py (nli_probs, embed_cos);\n- pilot_metrics.py (pilot_one, compute_pilot, rerun_jaccard);\n- analysis.py (ClusterBoot, boot_auc, auc_w, delong, tie_rate, at_threshold, combo_oof);\n- budget.py (Budget with the reservation INSIDE the semaphore, the fixed version);\n- local_llm.py (the Qwen3-8B verbaliser);\n- common.py (item_id, disable_triton_overrides);\n- labeller/fol.py + labeller/labeller.py (equivalent_modulo_vocab) + labeller/repair_census.py (edits(), fp(), symbols(), atoms()).\nFrom prereg.json take, verbatim:\n- the rubric A text and user_json, user_yesno and verbalise prompts, with their sha1s;\n- the models block;\n- max_tokens;\n- the thresholds rules;\n- combination_feature_sets.\nAlso:\n- data/invariance_items.json: 282 shared rewrites (+150 base items), for B2 invariance;\n- results/rewrite_scores.jsonl: the judges' existing rewrite scores, joined, not re-called;\n- results/analysis.json: the screen thresholds for the existing baselines (90th percentile of the oriented score on track-H CORRECT; judges P<0.5).\nNEGATIVE findings reused:\n- rt_reformalise_eq is closed (0.513), not re-run;\n- the pilot metrics are near-null (0.50-0.53), run once, no tuning;\n- the frontier gain vanished under disguise, which motivates analysis (c) here.\n\n(C) Exp C, /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_3/:\n- src/consensus.py sc_scores() and components()/entropy(), for the SC-5 scoring logic: eq_frac with missing/unparseable = not equivalent, plus entropy;\n- src/peers.py one_call() with the T>0 settings, for the SC-5 call pattern;\n- results/ SC-5 samples for screen sentences (if present), to compute SC-5 on the shared rewrite set with no new calls.\n\n(D) Exp A, /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_experiment_1/:\n- screen_items.json and href_items.json give the screen item_ids (track L 753 + H 302) on which B2 must be scored, so iteration 3 can fit it as a 5th fusion feature on exactly the ids PEER+TEXT is fitted on;\n- the track-H expert-corrected formulas are the primary B2 reader-gate items.\n\n(E) Sibling PEER+TEXT experiment (iter_2, running in parallel). This plan implements the strategy's E-POOL / R_AB / R_A / bootstrap definitions verbatim and the same label-vector sha1 function, so their per-item files join on metadata_item_id. The sibling also runs judge_cheap_disg on E. The two independent runs of the identical prompt give the judge test-retest number in iteration 3; do NOT share caches with the sibling.",
  "implementation_pseudocode": "LAYOUT (in the executor's workspace):\n- method.py orchestrator with --stage NAME [--limit N], one stage per step below, idempotent and cached;\n- src/ (copied exp D and exp C code, plus the new files e_pool.py, b2_world_probe.py, s4.py, analysis_E.py);\n- prereg_baselines.json + prereg_baselines.sha256;\n- data/;\n- results/scores/*.jsonl (append-only, one line per unit);\n- results/costs.jsonl (ledger);\n- results/analysis_E.json;\n- results/tables/*.csv;\n- folds_E.json;\n- E_baseline_features.jsonl;\n- method_out.json / full_method_out.json / mini_method_out.json / preview_method_out.json (exp_gen_sol_out; validate with aii-json; split with aii-file-size-limit if over the limit);\n- README.md;\n- .aii/manifest.yaml.\nSet PYTHONHASHSEED=0: repair_census.fp uses hash(), and determinism matters for the B2 mutant selection. Call common.disable_triton_overrides() before any torch op (torch 2.14 routes ops to Triton, which needs a C compiler). Put HF_HOME on the run's shared cache, as exp D did. Never pkill by name; manage background jobs by PID.\n\nSTAGE 0 — setup (≤30 min).\n- uv venv; install z3-solver, numpy, pandas, scikit-learn, scipy, statsmodels, torch, transformers, sentence-transformers, nltk, aiohttp, loguru. Copy exp D's nltk_data (wordnet) to data/.\n- Check the OpenRouter catalogue (GET /api/v1/models) for google/gemini-2.5-flash-lite, openai/gpt-4.1-nano and google/gemini-3.1-pro-preview, and record the prices in results/prices.json.\n- If the frontier id is gone, pick the listed successor in the same family and tier, logged in prereg.model_swaps. Run a 10-item cost/validity dry run on SCREEN items (exp D's track-L dev ids), never on E.\n- Check the key once with a $0.0001 call. If the shared key returns 402/limit errors, see the fallback plan.\n\nSTAGE 1 — e_pool.py (label-blind loader).\n- load_E(blind=True) reads the heldout_candidates rows and DELETES every label-bearing field: output, metadata_final_label, metadata_auto_label, metadata_label_tier, metadata_label_source, metadata_panel_votes, metadata_error_ops, metadata_repair_ops, metadata_repair_status, metadata_equiv_status, metadata_correct_not_equivalent, metadata_reading_choice, metadata_gold_audit_flag, metadata_reference_status, metadata_convention_flags, metadata_addrop_only_suspect, metadata_solver_lenient_label, metadata_panel_ambiguous. It keeps the ids, the text, candidate_fol, reference_fol (not used by any baseline except the B2 gate), the system fields, strata and disguised fields.\n- Every scoring stage imports only load_E(blind=True).\n- load_E(blind=False) raises unless prereg_baselines.sha256 exists AND its mtime is earlier than every results/scores/*.jsonl first-line timestamp. Exception: the frame-drawing script in Stage 2b.\n- Define pools exactly as the strategy says:\n  - E-POOL = system_class=='llm';\n  - GOLDSYS = malls_gpt4_gold rows;\n  - CCG = ccg2lambda rows, scored but reported separately;\n  - PRIMARY = parseable (candidate_fol non-empty and parsed by labeller/fol.py);\n  - R_AB = tier in {A, B} and final_label in {CORRECT, ERROR}, excluding CONTESTED, reading_choice and UNRESOLVED;\n  - R_A = tier A only (the card's 'A_unaudited_ref' 53 rows are EXCLUDED from R_A and listed);\n  - COVERAGE VIEW = R_AB ∪ UNPARSEABLE-as-ERROR;\n  - SENSITIVITY views: CONTESTED→CORRECT, CONTESTED→ERROR, ALL_TIERS (A+B+C), L25 without the top-up.\n- label_vector_sha1(ids, labels) = sha1 of the sorted lines 'item_id:label', the same function as the sibling.\n\nSTAGE 2 — prereg_baselines.json (written BEFORE any label read; sha256 file written next to it). It contains:\n- the models, prices, prompts and their sha1s (copied from exp D);\n- the feature list per combination set:\n  - S1 = [parse_fail, pilot_joint_conflict, pilot_arity_incons, pilot_shape_incons, pilot_dangling, pilot_undeclared, pilot_rerun_jacc];\n  - S2 = [rt_nli_min, rt_nli_fwd, rt_nli_bwd, rt_nli_contra, rt_embed_cos, rt_nli_min_local, rt_embed_cos_local];\n  - S3 = [judge_cheap_disg, judge_cheap_orig, judge_cheap2_disg, judge_cheap2_orig];\n  - SC = [sc5_eq_frac, sc5_entropy];\n  - S4 = S1 ∪ S2 ∪ S3 ∪ SC (primary);\n  - S4_iter1 = the exp D set minus rt_reformalise_eq (secondary; a bridge to the screen number);\n  - S5 = S4 + judge_strong_orig/disg (300 subset only, descriptive).\n  Model: L2 logistic regression, C=1.0, standardised within the training folds, missing → 0 plus a missing-indicator (combo_oof as in exp D).\n- Folds: fold(sentence_id) = int(sha1('E_folds_v1|'+sentence_id),16) % 5, over ALL 700 sentences. This is deterministic, independent of the sklearn version, and written to folds_E.json as {sentence_id: fold} and {item_id: fold}.\n- Thresholds, frozen from the SCREEN:\n  - for every baseline that exp D had, copy exp D's screen threshold value (results/analysis.json thresholds);\n  - judges: flag if P(faithful) < 0.5;\n  - B2: the threshold is the 90th percentile of b2_score on screen track-H CORRECT (corrected) items, computed in Stage 7a before any E label is read;\n  - S4: its threshold is nested (within each training fold, the OOF-free training-fit score quantile giving FA=0.10 on training CORRECT rows), applied to the held-out fold.\n- Bootstrap: 2,000 resamples, cluster = metadata_sentence_id, seed 0, percentile CIs.\n- Testability table: copied from dataset_card §3; untestable cells are declared and never tested.\n- The analysis list (a)-(k) below, the B2 spec, the budget caps per component, and the frontier sampling rule (2b).\n- Contents that iteration 3 needs: fold rule, feature lists, label-vector function.\n\nSTAGE 2b — frontier frame (draw_frontier_frame.py, the only script allowed to read labels pre-scoring; it reads only item_id, final_label, tier, source_stratum and sentence_id).\n- Cells = {CORRECT, ERROR} × {L25, L20, EXC, CTRL} within R_AB ∩ PRIMARY ∩ E-POOL.\n- 300 rows: 38 per cell (37 in 4 cells), sha1(item_id+'frontier_v1') order, at most 1 row per sentence per cell, and at most 2 rows per sentence overall.\n- Record per-cell population sizes → inclusion probabilities.\n- Write data/frontier_frame.json and put its sha256 in prereg.\n- This read is disclosed in prereg.freeze_context.\n\nSTAGE 3 — CPU baselines (ProcessPool, 7 workers; run in the background while the API stages run).\n(3a) parse_fail = 1 if labeller/fol.py cannot parse candidate_fol (or it is empty). Cross-tabulate against the dataset's UNPARSEABLE label (it should be identical; report the mismatches).\n(3b) Pilot metrics via compute_pilot(rows).\n- Documents: CTRL rows grouped by (system|variant, FOLIO story id if available in heldout_sentences, else sha1 bucket); MALLS rows grouped by (system|variant, source_stratum, int(sha1(sentence_id),16)%B), where B is chosen so buckets hold about 20 sentences.\n- Log the grouping in results/pilot_mapping_E.md.\n- rerun_jaccard: zero-shot vs few-shot predicate-set Jaccard for the Llama-3.3-70B and Qwen3-235B slots; cross-system Jaccard against the other slots of the same sentence for the rest (column pilot_rerun_jacc, a flag column says which).\n- No tuning.\n(3c) After Stage 4's SC samples exist: SC-5 scoring with a pair cache keyed (sha1(cand), sha1(sample)):\n- equivalent_modulo_vocab(cand, sample) with a z3 timeout of 2 s per call and the labeller's deterministic budget;\n- sc5_eq_frac = #equivalent / 5 (a timeout or unparseable sample = not equivalent, as in iteration 1);\n- sc5_entropy via components();\n- n_timeouts logged.\n- Oriented score: 1 - sc5_eq_frac.\n- Dedupe identical candidate strings within a sentence before calling z3.\n\nSTAGE 4 — API sweeps (asyncio, aiohttp, semaphore 24-32, Budget.call ledger after EVERY call, per-component caps; every call cached in results/llm_cache.jsonl keyed by (component, model, messages, params)).\nBefore each sweep, run a 20-item dry run to measure $/call, extrapolate, and refuse the sweep if the estimate exceeds its cap. Order and caps:\n(4a) judge_cheap orig + disg on E-POOL ∪ GOLDSYS ∪ CCG PRIMARY rows (about 6.84k LLM + 550 gold + 33 CCG).\n- Rubric A, judge_json, max_tokens 150, T=0, error-type field kept.\n- Disguised inputs = exp D disguise.disguise_item(text, fol) with default seed (the same call as the sibling). Assert the AST-inverse check per item; failures are logged and fall back to E's metadata_disguised_text/fol, with a flag.\n- Oriented score = 1 - faithful_prob/100.\n- JSON failures get one retry (as in judges.py), then the fallback 0.5 (logged count).\n- Cap $1.8. Estimate: about 14.9k calls × $0.00008 ≈ $1.2.\n(4b) judge_cheap2 (gpt-4.1-nano, user_yesno, logprobs, max_tokens 1) orig + disg on the same rows. Score = 1 - P(YES). Cap $0.9 (estimate $0.75).\n(4c) SC-5: gpt-4.1-nano, T=0.7, 5 samples per E sentence (700 × 5 = 3,500 calls).\n- Prompt = dataset E's prompts/fewshot_v1.txt, the same prompt the generators used, so SC samples live in the same notation. Log this as the choice; exp C used its own MALLS/FOLIO prompts.\n- Parse with judges.extract_formula.\n- Cap $0.7 (estimate $0.5).\n(4d) Round-trip API verbaliser: gemini-2.5-flash-lite, VERBALISE prompt, max_tokens 120, T=0, then clean_verbalisation(), on all PRIMARY rows. The disguised verbalisation of the disguised formula is secondary and runs only if the budget allows after 4e. Cap $0.5.\n(4e) judge_strong orig + disg on the 300 frame rows. Model and params from Stage 0 (reasoning effort low). Record per-call usage.cost and latency. Cap $3.0 (estimate 600 × $0.0035 ≈ $2.1). Run the 20-row dry run first.\n(4f) Test-retest: judge_cheap_disg re-called on 200 sha1-chosen E rows with a cache-bypass nonce in the CACHE KEY ONLY (the prompt is identical). Report Spearman and exact-match. Cap $0.05.\n(4g) B2 reader calls (Stage 7). Cap $2.0.\nHARD STOP when the cumulative ledger reaches $9.5; write budget_state.json after each call. The reservation happens inside the semaphore (exp D fix).\n\nSTAGE 5 — GPU round-trip (after 4d, and in parallel with the API stages).\n(5a) Local verbaliser Qwen3-8B via local_llm.py (bf16, batched greedy, the same VERBALISE prompt), then clean_verbalisation() (the local model wraps its answer as 'The formula X says ...').\n- First measure throughput on 64 rows.\n- If the full PRIMARY set would take more than 70 min, run a pre-declared stratified subsample of 3,000 rows (sha1 order within stratum × system) and log it.\n(5b) NLI with MoritzLaurer/DeBERTa-v3-large-mnli-fever-anli-ling-wanli (fp16, batch 32) in both directions, for both verbalisers:\n- rt_nli_fwd = 1 - P(entail | text ⇒ verbalisation);\n- rt_nli_bwd = 1 - P(entail | verbalisation ⇒ text);\n- rt_nli_min = 1 - min(P_fwd, P_bwd);\n- rt_nli_contra = max P(contradiction).\n- Alternate checkpoint cross-encoder/nli-deberta-v3-large → rt_nli_min_alt.\n(5c) all-mpnet-base-v2 cosine → rt_embed_cos = 1 - cos.\n\nSTAGE 6 — S4 (s4.py).\n- build_feature_table(): one row per E item_id (ALL rows, all systems), all oriented baseline columns, plus missing indicators, parse_fail, fold. Written to E_baseline_features.jsonl.\n- fit_s4_oof(table, label_series, folds, feats, C=1.0) → OOF p for every row. For fold k, fit on the rows of the other folds that have a label in the given regime, then predict ALL rows of fold k.\n- Emit:\n  - S4_oof_RAB (trained on R_AB labels);\n  - S4_oof_RAB_iter1set;\n  - S4_oof_ALLTIERS (sensitivity);\n  - S4_thr_flag (nested FA=0.10 threshold);\n  - the per-fold coefficients (results/s4_coefs.json).\n- The function is importable, so iteration 3 can call fit_s4_oof with R_ADJ labels and nest [S4 + PEER+TEXT] on identical folds.\n- Self-check: re-running with shuffled labels gives an OOF AUROC of about 0.5.\n\nSTAGE 7 — B2 world_probe (b2_world_probe.py). world_probe(text, fol, item_key) → {b2_score, b2_mismatch_det, b2_undet_share, n_worlds, worlds:[{mutant_op, direction, phi_truth, world_text, verdict}], status}.\nSteps:\n(i) Parse φ with labeller/fol.py into the repair_census tuple AST.\n(ii) Mutants: list(repair_census.edits(φ, φ)) filtered to ops {NEG, REV, QUANT, RESTR, CONN, DROP, SWAP, SCOPE}.\n- ADD and BIND are excluded: ADD yields nothing with target=φ, and BIND explodes combinatorially.\n- Drop mutants whose fp() equals fp(φ).\n- Order by sha1(item_key|op|repr(ψ)). Take round-robin over op types until 6 are z3-verified non-equivalent to φ (2 s timeout each; at most 20 candidates tried).\n(iii) Worlds.\n- Ground φ and ψ over a finite domain D = constants(φ∪ψ) + k fresh individuals, with k = 1..3 and |D| ≤ 4, or constants+1 if there are more than 3 constants, capped at 6. ∀ becomes a conjunction and ∃ a disjunction over D. Each ground atom is a z3 Bool.\n- Mutant i uses the direction φ∧¬ψ (φ TRUE) if i is even and ψ∧¬φ (φ FALSE) if i is odd, falling back to the other direction if unsat.\n- Solve with z3 Optimize, minimising the number of true atoms (soft ¬atom), timeout 2 s. Start with the smallest domain that is SAT.\n- This guarantees a mix of φ-TRUE and φ-FALSE worlds, so a reader that always says TRUE scores 0.5 mismatch.\n(iv) Verbalise by fixed template (no LLM).\n- 'There are exactly N individuals: <constant names split from camelCase/underscores> and thing 1..k.'\n- One line per TRUE ground atom of φ's predicates only. Unary: '<ind> — <pred words>: yes'. n-ary: '<pred words>: holds for (<ind1>, <ind2>) in that order'.\n- Closing line: 'Every property or relation not listed as holding is false for these individuals.'\n- Predicate words = camelCase/underscore split, lowercased.\n(v) Reader: gemini-2.5-flash-lite, T=0, one call per item with the item's worlds enumerated.\n- Prompt: 'Sentence: {text}. Below are N separate situations. For each, answer whether the sentence is TRUE, FALSE, or UNDETERMINED in that situation (UNDETERMINED only if the situation does not contain enough information). Treat each situation as the whole world. Return JSON {\"answers\": [..]}'. It NEVER sees a formula.\n- Unbatched check: 200 screen items with 1 world per call, to measure the batching effect (report agreement).\n(vi) Scores:\n- b2_score (primary, oriented) = (#determined verdicts ≠ φ-truth + 0.5 × #UNDETERMINED) / n_worlds;\n- b2_mismatch_det = mismatch among determined verdicts;\n- b2_undet_share, reported separately as the dropped-condition signal;\n- per-op mismatch.\n- Status is one of ok / no_mutant / z3_fail / reader_fail. Non-ok → coverage failure with fallback score 0.5, counted.\nORDER of execution:\n- 7a: the screen items (exp A screen_items + href_items, L 753 + H 302 by exp A item_id) → results/scores/b2_screen.jsonl.\n- 7b: the B2 gate + threshold.\n- 7c: the 282 shared rewrites + 150 base items (FA and flip per family) → b2_rewrites.jsonl.\n- 7d: all E PRIMARY rows in sha1(item_id) order until the $2 cap → b2_E.jsonl, with the covered-subset list logged.\nREADER GATE (7b), before 7d:\n- Worlds from formulas believed faithful: screen track-H expert-CORRECTED formulas (primary gate), and E references with reference_status in {GOLD_PANEL_OK, TRUSTED_AGREED, PANEL_REPAIRED}, reported per stratum L25/L20/EXC/CTRL. These are references, not candidate labels.\n- Gate metric = balanced accuracy of the verdict vs φ-truth over TRUE/FALSE worlds, with UNDETERMINED counted wrong. Pass if ≥0.85 on the primary gate.\n- Prompt dev: at most 2 variants on a 40-item screen-H dev slice, which is excluded from the gate; the choice is frozen in prereg (append-only prereg_b2.json, sha256).\n- If the gate fails, B2 is still scored as pre-registered but marked NOT_ELIGIBLE_FOR_FUSION.\nIf B2 crashes irrecoverably (parse/grounding blow-ups over 30% of items, or the reader fails over 20% of calls) after one fix attempt, write results/b2_status.json with the reason. B2 is then CLOSED.\n\nSTAGE 8 — analysis_E.py. It runs only after the prereg check passes; the label load happens here.\n(a) For every metric column, plus S4_oof and b2: AUROC [cluster CI], AUPRC with prevalence, and precision/recall/FA at the frozen threshold with prevalence stated, plus the tie rate. Regimes: R_AB PRIMARY (headline), R_A (pooled L20+EXC+CTRL tier A = the R_A headline; per stratum only where testable, else sign-only), COVERAGE VIEW, and the sensitivity views. Each is reported pooled, per source_stratum, for the long pool L25+L20+EXC, and for L25 with and without the top-up. GOLDSYS and CCG are reported separately.\n(b) Paired Δ (metric − judge_cheap_disg): cluster-bootstrap CI + DeLong p, under R_AB and R_A, pooled and per testable stratum. Also Δ(S4_oof − judge_cheap_disg).\n(c) Frontier on the 300 frame: paired Δ strong_orig − cheap_orig, strong_disg − cheap_disg and strong_orig − cheap_disg, both unweighted and IPW-weighted. Also the $ per call and the ratio to the cheap judge, checked against the $0.002 gate.\n(d) Contamination per LLM component (judge_cheap, judge_cheap2, judge_strong, rt API verbaliser if the disguised run exists, B2 reader not applicable because it is text-only; say so):\n- Δ_disg = AUROC_orig − AUROC_disg (paired);\n- DiD = Δ_disg on GOLDSYS rows (public gold as candidate, labelled by the blind gold audit) − Δ_disg on E-POOL rows;\n- gold-recognition probe: P(judge says faithful | GOLDSYS row, panel ERROR), orig − disg, compared with the same quantity on LLM ERROR rows;\n- MDE = 2.8 × bootstrap SE of each contrast (α=.05, 80% power).\n(e) Judge recall per error type at MATCHED FA (0.10 and 0.20) for orig vs disg, cheap vs cheap2 vs strong (strong on the frame only). Types come from tier-A metadata_repair_ops (single- and two-op errors) and tier-B metadata_error_ops (COMPOUND, MEANING_RENAME, ADD/DROP). The per-type CIs come from the bootstrap. Also report the judges' error_type field accuracy vs single-op tier-A repair ops, against a majority-class chance baseline.\n(f) Complexity.\n- AUROC per bin of words (<12, 12-19, 20-24, 25-29, 30-34, ≥35), n_quant, depth, n_conditions (1, 2, 3, 4+) and exception_type, for every metric. Cost: this slightly favours all metrics equally in the coverage view.\\n(9) Judge recall 'at matched FA' uses per-judge thresholds set on E CORRECT rows. This is a descriptive analysis, not a deployable threshold, and it is labelled so. Deployable precision uses the thresholds frozen on the screen.\\n(10) System-level τ over 13 rows is descriptive only.\\nNo lookups beyond the local artifacts were made in planning. The executor must confirm model ids and prices via OpenRouter /models before any sweep.\",\n  \"builds_on\": \"This is a DEEPEN iteration. Nothing is started fresh, and all code is reused from iteration 1.
